Body must include `kind: "paper" | "key" | "tags"` plus either { filters, options } or { questionIds, options } (mutually exclusive). `paper`/`key` return one Word `.docx` (filename `QP_<title>.docx` / `Answers_<title>.docx`) to institute staff and **a PDF to everyone else (2026-10-05)** — `resolveExportAccess`'s `format` picks, the PDF comes from `lib/export/pdf/` and `maxDuration` is 120 for the Chromium start; **`tags` (2026-06-14) returns an `.xlsx`** (`Tags_<title>.xlsx`) via SheetJS — the nda-tracker enrichment sheet (`buildTagRows`/`tagRowsToAoa` from `lib/export/tagsSheet`), no image fetch. No ZIP wrapper. Cart-mode preserves caller's id order. `options.includeSourceTag` (2026-07-28) is threaded into the `paper` branch only. **Download gate (2026-07-11):** `resolveExportAccess({kind,isSignedIn,isStaff})` rejects `paper`/`key` from anon (401) and `tags` from non-staff (403, 401 anon) — enforced server-side after payload validation, before the query. Rate tiers: anon 20/hr/IP (moot — blocked at the gate), **student 50/hr**, staff 200/hr (resolves `getSessionUser` alongside `getSessionMember` since a student has no org membership). See [[signup-gate-placement]] + the 2026-07-11 Decisions entry. **`mockSlug` (2026-10-07):** a third body shape, exclusive with filters and questionIds, paper/key only: the route loads the published past paper, refuses a chapter test or practice paper (400), refuses a paper whose questions do not all resolve (409, never served short), and passes `sectionOf` so both builders head the paper by its own sections; logged as `mode='mock'` with `mock_id` (migration 0136). **Safety net (2026-10-07):** `lib/export/paperFile.ts` serves the Word file when the PDF fails to print and logs `export pdf failed, serving docx`; the box names the file from Content-Type / Content-Disposition (`lib/export/fileType.ts`). `GET /api/export/access` (`no-store`) answers the viewer's download state for boxes on cached pages.
│   ├── api/bookmarks/                     POST (JSON, signed-in via `getSessionUser` — anon → 401). Body `{ questionId (uuid), bookmarked (bool) }` (zod). Own-row insert-or-delete on `question_bookmarks` (migration 0047) via `setBookmark`. Powers the `/browse` BookmarkButton + `/saved`.
│   ├── api/unsubscribe/[token]/           POST-ONLY (migration 0059; 2026-07-15) — sets `student_profiles.email_opt_out` for the row the token matches. **No auth: the token IS the capability** (someone who can't sign in must still be able to get out), so it's service-role. **POST, deliberately**: Gmail/Outlook link-scanners PREFETCH urls, so acting on GET would opt people out who never clicked — the GET surface is the `/unsubscribe/[token]` confirm page. Also serves RFC 8058 one-click (mailbox providers POST the `List-Unsubscribe` header here directly). Non-uuid → 400 (avoids a 22P02 cast error); unknown-but-valid uuid → 200, same as success, so it's not a token-probing oracle.
│   ├── unsubscribe/[token]/               the confirm page an email footer links to (2026-07-15) — `page.tsx` (noindex, no auth) + `UnsubscribeForm.tsx` (client island whose button POSTs `/api/unsubscribe/[token]`). Renders but NEVER acts, for the scanner-prefetch reason above. Smoke-tested end-to-end 2026-07-15 (GET left `email_opt_out` false; POST flipped exactly one row) — mandatory, since it builds `ƒ` and the build gate never renders those.
│   ├── api/notes/progress/                POST (JSON, signed-in — anon → 401). Body validated + normalized by pure `sanitizeProgressWrite` (bookmark / mastered / checkpoint self-score / last-viewed touch; rejects no-ops). Own-row upsert on `notes_progress` (migration 0046) via `saveOwnProgress`. Powers the notes track controls + "Your notes" strip.
│   ├── guide/                             Public, anon-friendly, Google-indexable strategy guides. page.tsx is a REGISTRY-DERIVED EXAM PICKER since 2026-08-22 (was a 307 to /guide/nda) — it lists every EXAM_REGISTRY entry with a non-null `guidesPath`, so a third exam's guides are a one-line registry change. It, /guide/nda and /guide/mht-cet all render `GuideShell` with the `/notes`-style exam rail (All exams · NDA · MHT-CET) from `lib/guide/guidesNav.ts`; before that they were the only pages in either tree with no side nav, and /guide/nda's card grid dropped 4-col → 2-col to make room for it. opengraph-image.tsx is a section-wide OG image (NDA-Maths-themed gradient) inherited by /guide/* children that don't override; /guide/nda-english/opengraph-image.tsx overrides with a purple English-themed gradient for that subtree. nda-maths/page.tsx landing also renders cross-link banners pointing into /notes/nda-maths/statistics AND /guide/nda-english. **The four exam hubs render `_components/GuideHubList` (2026-10-07):** the biggest guide as a Start-here card, the rest as one-tap rows by size, each card's blurb and highlights behind a row's arrow (kept on the page for search engines); pure core `lib/guide/hubList.ts`.
│   │   ├── reports/page.tsx               **Trend-reports index (2026-09-29)** — the eleven `/guide/<subject>/trends` pages listed as dated, citable reports, grouped by exam: the claim each makes (with its number), the sitting its data runs through and its committed content date. Registry `lib/guide/trendsReports.ts` (asserted against the filesystem BOTH ways by `tests/trends-reports.test.ts`). Linked from the `/guide` picker; in the sitemap with `lastmod` = newest report date.
│   │   ├── _components/                   Shared `/guide` primitives:
│   │   │       · ReportProvenance — the line under a trends page's hero (2026-09-29): "Report · Updated <content date> · Data through <sitting> · N papers · N questions · every count is a public question →". Paper/question counts are passed by the page from its OWN stat block (never typed twice); a missing date prints nothing rather than the build date. Every trends page now titles its hero with the registry `claim` and renders this block.

│   │   │       · GuideSideNav — same `landingHref` prop (was hard-coded to nda-maths)
│   │   │       · GuideHero · StatBlock · BrowseLink (resolves to /browse via buildBrowseUrl) · PrevNextNav · ChapterCard · CompoundCard
│   │   │       · DriftTable — accepts `rowLabel` prop (defaults "Principle"; english trends passes "Chapter")

│   │   │       · **RelatedPlaybooks** — ONE generic cross-link card row for every guide (props: `guidePath` + `items` (a guide's PLAYBOOKS or maths TOP_11) + `slugs` + optional `pathSegment`/`heading`/`intro`). Replaced the 8 former per-guide copies (RelatedPrinciples + Related{English,Physics,Chemistry,Biology,Geography,History,Polity}Playbooks) on 2026-06-01 — they differed only by an import path + one href. Maths passes `pathSegment="principles"`. Exports a pure `relatedItemHref` for unit testing.

TIME_SPLIT mirrors Physics (35 Chemistry / 55 Physics, a stated budget). Trends carry `easyQ`: 2025's shift was EASY 56-60% → 42%, invisible in %HARD. Tests: tests/guide-mht-cet-chemistry-playbooks.test.ts (grid-pinned; live HARD counts; one practice-only subtopic excluded by name AND proven PYQ-free) · tests/mhtcet-chemistry-trends-reconcile.test.ts.

Year grain because rates are a chapter's SHARE of its year scaled to 25 — papers changed from 30 to 25 questions in 2025. Playbook deep-dives, formulas and traps were drafted by parallel agents and reviewed. Tests: tests/jee-mains-maths-guide-data.test.ts (tier membership pinned, slugs resolve both ways, no figures in prose, callout directions) + tests/jee-trends-matrix.test.ts (offline) · tests/guide-jee-mains-maths-playbooks.test.ts (live: names resolve, every PYQ-bearing subtopic drilled).

Organic Reaction Mechanisms sits under `PLAYBOOK_LINE` (0.5 a paper) and the 8 chapters that left the syllabus (no notes) are listed, not drilled. For student self-study (scrollable, indexable) + digital-board teaching. **Two-tier hub (2026-06-08):** `page.tsx` is a real cross-exam index (was a redirect) and **`[examSlug]/page.tsx`** is a dynamic per-exam hub (`/notes/nda`·`/notes/mht-cet`·`/notes/jee-mains`) listing that exam's notes subjects with an honest "coming soon" empty state — both derive from `lib/notes/notesNav.ts` (`getNotesExamGroups`/`getNotesExamGroup`). Below them, `nda-maths/page.tsx` + nda-physics + mht-cet-maths + **nda-biology/page.tsx** are real per-subject CollectionPages listing every shipped chapter as cards with live per-chapter PYQ counts via `getNotesTaxonomy`. The dynamic `[examSlug]` coexists with the concrete subject folders (concrete route wins). ISR-cached (`export const revalidate = 3600` on every page; cookie-free `createSupabaseAnonClient` so caching survives). Prod warm-state ~200ms.
│       ├── _types.ts                      `SubtopicNote = { subtopicName, title, oneLineDefinition, whyItMatters, concepts: ConceptUnit[], related? }`. **`ConceptUnit` is a discriminated union** (`ConceptUnitFormula | ConceptUnitReference`, gated by `kind: "formula" | "reference"`) — both variants share `slug, name, intuition, definition, pyqExampleId?, selfCheckExample? (independent attempt, full solution behind one reveal), practiceSet? (PracticeProblem[] — Level 1 mastery reps: prompt + answer + optional one-line method), visualizationSlug? (one of 69 SVG diagrams — static or interactive), traps?` and differ only on the core teaching slot: **formula variant** carries `formula?` (FormulaSpec) + required `authoredExample`; **reference variant** carries required `table: ReferenceTable` (2–5 columns × ≥1 row, optional per-row `noteAmber` + `pyqExampleId` for `[Q]` chips, optional table-level `caption`). **Per-concept drill lists are NOT on this type** — they live in `question_concept_tags` (migration 0021) and are resolved at request time via `loadResolvedDrills`. **Seven plain-text fields render WITHOUT KaTeX OR Markdown** (plain text + SEO metadata): the five SubtopicNote/ConceptUnit fields `title`, `oneLineDefinition`, `whyItMatters`, `concept.name`, `formula.label` PLUS the two ChapterNote fields `chapter.title` + `chapter.intro` (GuideHero subtitle, subject-landing cards, JSON-LD, `<meta description>`) — keep LaTeX **and** `**bold**` / `- ` bullets out of all of them; gated by `npm run notes:latex` (Markdown + ChapterNote checks added 2026-05-30). Reference variant adds **`table.columns[]`** to the plain-text-no-LaTeX list (notes-latex-audit enforces). (The faded-example rung was removed 2026-05-29 — it was broken, with the answer always visible.)
│       ├── print/[subjectRoute]/[chapterSlug]/page.tsx   **printable per-chapter handout** (2026-08-19) — `/notes/print/<subjectRoute>/<chapterSlug>`, the whole chapter as one static A4 document for the browser's Save-as-PDF. ONE dynamic route for all 83 chapters (no per-chapter wrapper — nothing here is per-chapter); `generateStaticParams` prerenders every one, so a handout serves `x-nextjs-cache: HIT` and costs zero DB reads at request time. **`robots: noindex`** is load-bearing — it near-duplicates the canonical subtopic pages and this project has taken a GSC duplicate-canonical hit; deliberately NOT robots.txt-Disallowed (that would hide the noindex). Not in the sitemap (it enumerates explicitly). See the 2026-08-19 Decisions log.
│       ├── _components/                   **`NotesChapterLanding` + `NotesSubtopicPage` + `NotesSubjectLanding`** — registry-driven page renderers; the per-chapter AND per-subject route files are thin wrappers that delegate here (a subject index page is just `<NotesSubjectLanding subjectRoute=… />` + `buildSubjectMetadata(route)`, fully derived from `NOTES_CHAPTERS` — 2026-06-08, replaced 4 hand-written ~130-line pages). Each renderer exports a `build{Chapter,Subtopic,Subject}Metadata` helper. · `ConceptUnitCard` (read-mode workhorse — branches on `concept.kind`: intuition → definition → formula? → visualization? → **worked example OR reference table** → **collapsed "Practice this concept" disclosure wrapping self-check + Level 1 reps** → bank PYQ → traps → footer row (always-present "Report this concept" affordance + "Drill N more →" when the concept has tagged questions) — the drill link carries `from`/`fromLabel` (this subtopic's URL + `#concept-slug`) so /browse can return to the exact card via the `BackToNotes` pill) · `WorkedExampleAuthored` (formula variant, always-visible worked steps) · `ReferenceTableBlock` (reference variant, bordered N-column table; `compact` prop for revision-sheet use; amber-tinted rows when `noteAmber` is set; per-row `[Q]` chip when row carries `pyqExampleId`) · `SelfCheckCard` (sky; prompt + one Show-solution reveal) · `PracticeSet` (violet; Level 1 reps with per-rep answer+method reveal) · `SubtopicMasteryCheckpoint` (emerald; 5 interleaved bank PYQs via WorkedExampleCard + `pickInterleavedCheckpoint`) · `SubtopicSummary` (per-subtopic auto-derived formula + reference-table + trap recap via `deriveSummary` — reference tables surface in a "Reference tables (N)" section as per-concept disclosures, the full table inline) · `ChapterRevisionSheet` (collapsible chapter-wide sheet, same three-section structure as SubtopicSummary, auto-derived) · `ConceptWeightTable` (collapsible chapter-overview triage table — per-concept PYQ count + % of chapter PYQs, grouped by subtopic, sorted high-yield-first, foundations muted; fed by one RLS-scoped `question_concept_tags` query in `NotesChapterLanding`) · `FormulaBlock` · `TrapCallout` · `NotesPaywall` (preview-gate wall on a `tier:"paid"` chapter — renders in place of the locked concepts + checkpoint; anon → sign up/in, signed-in → /pricing) · **`PracticeGate` (2026-07-12)** — client wrapper (skeleton→wall/children via `useSignedIn` + pure `practiceGateState`) around the interactive practice: the per-concept "Practice this concept" disclosure body (`compact` variant) + `SubtopicMasteryCheckpoint` (`full` variant). Signed-in → the practice; anon → a "sign in to practice" wall. **Client-side so notes pages stay ISR-static** (a server auth read would de-cache the corpus; the checkpoint PYQs are public — a nudge, not a boundary; see [[signup-gate-placement]]). Structural (in the shared renderers) → every future chapter inherits it · **`NotesProgressControls`** — signed-in-only bookmark + mark-mastered chips (below the chapter chips) + a fire-and-forget last-viewed touch on mount · **`CheckpointSelfScore`** — self-report row inside the checkpoint (reveal-based, not auto-graded) → saves the latest score · **`YourNotesStrip`** — signed-in-only client island on the `/notes` index (continue · bookmarked · mastered), hrefs built from the denormalized `notes_progress` row. All three write via `POST /api/notes/progress`, read via the browser client (own-row RLS); client helpers in `notes/_components/progressClient.ts`. See [[project-notes-signin]] · `ReportConceptDialog` (client island in the concept-card footer; logged-in form — category + details — POSTs `/api/notes/reports`; **resolves auth client-side via the browser supabase client so free notes pages stay ISR-cached** (no server cookie read); anon → sign-in prompt + concept-prefilled mailto to `connect.lwspune@gmail.com`) · `visualizations/` — **107 hand-rolled SVG diagrams** (run `npm run stats` for the live count) dispatched by `concept.visualizationSlug`. Per-chapter diagram choices — which concepts get one + static-vs-rotatable — live in each chapter's `_data` + the Decisions log, NOT enumerated here (it drifted; the [[notes-diagram-decision-rule]] memory + `npm run stats` are the sources of truth). **All SVG, no 3D library** (three.js was evaluated + rejected for bundle size / mobile perf / visual consistency). Static figures are server components; interactive + rotatable ones are `"use client"` with `useState`. Rotatable "3-D" uses an orthographic turntable projection that **follows the cursor** (`az -= dx`, `el += dy`). Conventions: indigo wrapper + `max-w-md mx-auto`; SVG `fill` must use a standard opacity step (a non-generated step like `/8` silently falls back to SVG-default black). **Add one:** build the component → add its slug to the `VisualizationSlug` union in `_types.ts` → add a `case` in `renderVisualization` (ConceptUnitCard) → set `visualizationSlug` on the concept in its `_data`. ConceptUnitCard renders the `definition` field via `RichText` (bullets + bold), other prose via `KatexRenderer` (now bold-aware).
│       ├── _components/print/         handout renderer — `NotesChapterPrint` (server-only; cover + contents + every subtopic + a closing formula/trap revision sheet) · `printStyles.ts` (A4 `@page` CSS, inlined as a `<style>` tag so it can never leak into on-screen surfaces) · `PrintButton` (the only client JS; `window.print()`, hidden by `@media print`). **Re-composes the same `_data` modules rather than restyling the subtopic page** — the four reveal components (`SelfCheckCard`/`PracticeSet`/`WorkedExampleAuthored`/`SubtopicMasteryCheckpoint`) hide answers behind `useState` and CSS cannot open React state, so the handout renders them open. Shares the leaf renderers (`KatexRenderer`/`RichText`/`BlockText`/`renderVisualization`), so content cannot drift. Two print-CSS rules earned the hard way: do NOT `break-inside: avoid` a concept card (taller than a page ⇒ strands ~80% of one), and restate `list-style` (Tailwind preflight resets `ol` → unnumbered solution steps).
│       ├── _components/NotesHandoutLink.tsx   sign-in-gated "Download as PDF" chip on the chapter landing. Client-gated via `useSignedIn` + the pure `practiceGateState` (skeleton → sign-in link → download link) so the landing stays ISR-static; a server session read would de-cache it. A **conversion nudge, not a boundary** — `/notes/print/…` stays reachable signed-out, matching `PracticeGate` and [[signup-gate-placement]].

│       ├── cds-maths/number-system/  12 subtopics · 71 concepts (66 formula + 5 reference) · 85 traps · 218 q · 100% coverage. **First CDS notes chapter and the first `cds-maths` subject** (`/notes/cds` was a coming-soon hub until 2026-09-15). The biggest chapter in CDS Elementary Mathematics (~11 of every 100 questions, all 20 sittings 2016-II…2026-I). **Phase-D reshape, and the reshape was TWO-SIDED**: `catalog.json` was seeded from three OTHER banks' taxonomies to CLASSIFY a paper, never to sequence teaching, so the names were technique-canonical but the cut was wrong — the 71-q "Divisibility and Remainders" bundled four unrelated technique families and two subtopics held ONE question each (one of them mislabelled: the lone "Trailing Zeros" row is solved by parity, while the real Legendre cluster sat under Primes). 12 classification subtopics → 12 teaching subtopics, 212 rows reparented, and **`catalog.json` + the 20 `<paper>.questions.json` files rewritten alongside the DB** (`scripts/cds-maths/reshape-number-system.ts`, idempotent, dry-run by default) because subtopic is SOFT-validated and AUTO-CREATED, so a DB-only reshape would be silently undone by a re-commit. The 50 `b*.json` bands are deliberately NOT rewritten (transcription evidence); `strictSubtopics: true` at `commit.ts` is what makes a stale band name fail loudly instead. Teaching arc: primitives → how a number is WRITTEN → how it is BUILT → the hard end. Difficulty follows it unprompted (the two densest HARD units, Factorisation 38% and Congruences 27%, land at positions 10 and 9). Pages: foundations (7 concepts·17 q, incl. a reference unit on the THREE CDS statement formats — 55 of the chapter's 218 q) · place-value (8·26) · divisibility-rules (4·9) · unit-digit (5·13) · primes (6·22) · factors-divisors (6·20) · hcf-lcm-laws (7·29, biggest) · hcf-lcm-applications (7·20, the three remainder recipes) · congruences (6·26) · factorisation (6·16) · squares-cubes (6·14) · rational-irrational (3·6). `cds-ns-` subtopic slugs + `cdsns-` concept slugs (NDA Maths already owns Statistics/Circles/Logarithms/Quadratic Equations chapter names). **2 new SVGs** — `cds-unit-digit-cycle-wheel` (the wrap-to-the-LAST-node trap) + `cds-rational-irrational-line` (a zoom showing decimals bracketing √2 without landing). Near-zero diagram load is correct here: Number System is non-spatial, so the chapter leans on reference tables instead. 12 rows carry a recorded defect or ambiguity and are excluded from `pyqExampleId` duty, except in the one concept whose subject IS inconsistent data. Authored serially, not by parallel agents; every hand-authored answer re-derived (3 were wrong and fixed).

├── lib/
│   ├── supabase/authUsers.ts    `listAllAuthUsers` — every auth user, PAGED (one `listUsers` page returns 1,000 and no error). Extracted from `batches/invitesAdmin.ts` 2026-09-27 when `/dashboard/feedback` needed it; the remaining single-page callers are on the ROADMAP backfill ledger.
│   ├── batches/studentLogins.ts + studentLoginsAdmin.ts + api/batches/students/ + dashboard/batches/[id]/roster/StudentLoginsCard.tsx   **staff-created student logins (2026-10-06).** A teacher/admin makes a login for a student who has none (one at a time or a pasted class list: `name, email[, password]`, commas or spreadsheet tabs), the student joins the batch at once and the credentials come back ONCE for Copy / Download sheet (never stored). Pure core `studentLogins.ts` (`parseStudentLines` · `generatePassword` without 0/O/1/l/I · `credentialsCsv` formula-safe · `credentialsText`; TDD `tests/student-logins.test.ts`); service `createStudentLogins` authorizes by reading the batch through the CALLER's RLS client, creates confirmed accounts (`user_metadata` name · `signup_source: "staff"` · `created_by_staff` · `created_for_batch`), enrolls, deletes the account if enrolling fails, and sends an EXISTING account an ordinary invite instead of touching it (`tests/batch-student-logins.test.ts`). Route: requireEditor + 10 requests/hour/staff, 100 students/request.
│   ├── supabase/{client,server,middleware,admin}.ts    four supabase-js variants. `server.ts` exports BOTH `createSupabaseServerClient` (cookie-aware, default for /browse + /dashboard + admin routes) AND `createSupabaseAnonClient` (cookie-free anon client — opts the caller out of forced dynamic rendering, used by /notes pages so `revalidate=3600` ISR actually caches).
│   ├── auth.ts                            getSessionUser, getSessionMember, requireAdmin, **requireEditor** (ADMIN or TEACHER — added 2026-05-26 for migration 0025), HttpError
│   ├── auth/credentials.ts                client-safe validators (isValidEmail, isValidPassword, MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH, validateSignup) — extracted from members/admin.ts so the /signup client bundle never imports the service-role client. members/admin.ts re-exports for back-compat.
│   ├── entitlements/{access,query,admin}.ts   per-user premium access (migration 0026). access.ts: pure isEntitlementActive + hasActiveScope + SCOPE_ALL. query.ts: loadEntitlements + userHasAccess (RLS-scoped, fail-closed). admin.ts: service-role grantEntitlement (require-signup-first, source='comp') + listEntitlements (email-hydrated) + revokeEntitlement + pure validateGrant.
│   ├── books/{order,registry,query,sync,curate,service}.ts  the books data layer. `order.ts` (**pure, TDD** `tests/books-order.test.ts`): `sittingOrdinal` + `sittingLabel` + `buildChapterSections`. It exists because **the two exams need OPPOSITE sort keys** — NDA carries `pyq_month` on all 900 English PYQ rows (Apr = NDA 1, Sep = NDA 2) while its 18 source filenames follow eight different conventions, and CDS is the mirror image (`pyq_month` NULL on all 2,280, sitting legible only from a uniform `Eng_CDS_<year>_<sitting>.pdf`). Applying either rule to the other exam mis-orders it SILENTLY, and in NDA 2019 a filename sort actively inverts the sittings because `GAT_NDA2_2019...` sorts before `NDA1_2019...` — that real pair is the discriminating test case. Sets are the ordering unit and the comparator is TOTAL, so output depends only on input content, never arrival order. `registry.ts`: the book as DATA (subject + exams + ordered chapters); chapter order is EDITORIAL (heaviest-first) rather than a `count desc` sort, which would reshuffle the book on every ingest. Chapter names are matched against `chapters.name` and a near-miss yields an EMPTY chapter rather than an error — guarded by the prod-contract `tests/books-registry.test.ts`, which fails in BOTH directions (a registry name that stopped resolving; a bank chapter absent from the registry, the one that loses content quietly). `query.ts`: two-phase fetch (ordering metadata → `queryQuestionsByIds`) like /browse, `.range()`-paged under the 1000-row cap AND `.in()`-chunked at 200 ids — **different limits, only one of which is 1000**; Vocabulary is already 773. It reads ORDER from `book_questions` (service-role — those tables are RLS-locked) and the bank only for what a stored row does not carry (set id, year, sitting). `sync.ts` (**pure, TDD** `tests/books-sync.test.ts`): the bank-vs-book diff. It only ever ADDS — new is APPENDED after its section's max (never inserted mid-order, which would reshuffle a hand-arranged sequence); gone is REPORTED, never deleted (deleting would take the `excluded` decision with it and re-add the question next run); and **re-chaptered is REPORTED, never applied**, because the book's `chapter_slug` is deliberately allowed to diverge from the bank's — that divergence IS the "move this question" feature. `curate.ts` (**pure, TDD** `tests/books-curate.test.ts`): `planSetMove`/`planSetToSection` — the unit of movement is the SET, never the question, because `papers/sections.ts`'s `positionForMove` shifts ONE item and a question dragged out of its passage is stranded from the thing that makes it answerable. It clears a neighbour ENTIRELY rather than swapping one position, and spreads a block evenly into whatever gap remains. Fractional positions have finite precision — ~50 moves into the SAME gap exhaust it and nothing renormalises. `service.ts` (server-only, service-role): each write RE-DERIVES the chapter from the same functions the reader uses, so a stale tab either finds its set where the server sees it or not at all — never moves the wrong thing.
│   ├── formula/{types,index,filters,query}.ts + <chapter>.ts   the formula axis. `types.ts` — `FormulaTopic` (slug · **kind**: formula|property|technique, because `A^T = A` is a property and cofactor expansion is a technique · latex · plain-text `statement` feeding <meta description> · symbols · questionIds) + `FormulaChapter`. `index.ts` — `FORMULA_CHAPTERS` (add a chapter here and the pages, index and sitemap all follow), `formulaBySlug`, `topicsByWeight`, `relatedTopics` (co-occurrence over shared questions). `filters.ts` — pure exam/kind filtering, TDD `tests/formula-filters.test.ts`; kind comes from `question_kind`, never from whether a `pyq_year` is set. `query.ts` — `loadPracticeIds`, a narrow read rather than widening `QuestionRow`, which four other surfaces share. `matrices-determinants.ts` is GENERATED (`npm run formula:generate`) from the hand tags — never hand-edit the id lists.

See [[collaborative-paper-builder]].

UI: `components/premium/PremiumLimitCard`, `components/reveal/RevealDailyLimit`, `components/performance/ProjectionGate`

Counts are NOT here — each page passes its own to `ReportProvenance`.

A test walks every key of `Filters` so a new field cannot slip through. `shouldShowBrowseLanding({filters,isStaff})` — org members keep the live list (own-org PRIVATE rows). `buildExamStarters(catalog, examIds, countsBySlug)` — pills in registry order, dropping any exam with no questions in its DEFAULT view or no resolved UUID. `pickStarterChapters(landings,{perExam,total})` — densest chapters with a per-exam cap (a straight top-N would be an all-NDA-Maths list) and a name tiebreak so the panel is stable between builds.
│   ├── questions/defaultViewCounts.ts     **`getDefaultViewCountsByExam()` (2026-08-13)** — cached (`revalidate: 3600`) head count per exam on EXACTLY the basis `/browse?examId=…` defaults to (`practiceOnly ? 'practice' : 'pyq'`). Exists because the two nearby aggregates are both wrong for a pill that navigates: the exam catalog counts pyq **+** practice (would advertise NDA 8,259 against a destination of 4,860) and summing `listChapterLandings` drops every chapter under `MIN_QUESTIONS_FOR_LANDING` (rendered 4,699). Head counts only, so it is immune to the PostgREST 1000-row trap; a DB blip returns `{}` and the pills simply vanish rather than 500ing the bank's front door.
│   ├── questions/formatMix.ts             **`/browse` format-filter visibility (2026-08-16)**, pure + TDD `tests/format-mix.test.ts`. `shouldShowFormatFilter({mixedExamIds,examId,activeFormat})` — the Format control (All/MCQ/Written/Numeric, the `question_format` axis of migrations 0041 + 0061) renders only where the exam actually holds more than one format; five of eleven exams are 100% MCQ across 29,524 PUBLIC questions, where it could only ever be a no-op (same hidden-not-disabled posture as the syllabus-fit filter off JEE). **Its input is `EXAM_REGISTRY.mixedFormats`, NOT a live count, and that was measured**: grouping (exam, kind, format) over the bank is a 49,372-row seq scan (~4.4 s, 8,838 buffers) that exceeds the anon role's 3 s `statement_timeout` and timed out INTERMITTENTLY in a production build; per-exam splitting uses an index but still leaves JEE Mains at ~3.7 s. Serving it live would need a new index on the most heavily written table in the schema, or a raised timeout — to decide whether to draw a control. `get_format_mix()` survives as a service-role-only PROBE (migration 0079) whose one caller is the prod-contract `tests/format-mix-registry.test.ts`, which re-measures the flag against the live bank and fails in BOTH directions. **The two failure directions are deliberately asymmetric**: an active `?format=` pins the control ON, and every unknown resolves toward showing it — an inert control is noise, but a hidden one while the filter is active strands the viewer with an invisible narrowing. That asymmetry is what makes a hand-declared flag safe. `mixedFormatExamIds(examIds)` maps the flagged slugs through the cached `getExamIdMap()` (the page filters by uuid, the registry is keyed by slug), skipping any slug that did not resolve.

all other exams use pyq_note which carries day+shift). `QuestionRow` extended 2026-05-26 with `questionNumber + pyqYear + pyqMonth + pyqNote` (all nullable) threaded through both `queryQuestions` + `queryQuestionsByIds`. Also composes the PYQ citation on `/notes` + `/guide` `WorkedExampleCard` (via `loadWorkedExamples`' `provenance` field, 2026-05-29) — same convention, two surfaces.

TDD in `tests/return-to.test.ts`.

│   ├── sync/{paperPush,paperSittings}.ts  the vault→nda-tracker paper push. **`paperPush.ts`** is pure: `buildPaperPushPayload` (walks the SAME `groupBySet` loop as the docx + tags sheet, so Q-numbers match the printed paper and the Evalbee scan by construction), `trackerExamId(paperId, sittingNo)`, `pushTitle`, `planPush`, `pushDisabledReason`. **SITTINGS (0097, 2026-09-12):** one paper is routinely conducted more than once — batch A Monday, batch B Thursday — and each conduct owns its own date, batch and results, so each is its own tracker exam. Sitting 1 keeps the original unsuffixed `exam_vault_<paperId>` (nine drafts were live on it); sitting N>1 is `_s<N>`. `pushTitle` is load-bearing, not cosmetic: the tracker's Update Results modal edits date/marking/subject/batch/branch but **NOT `name`**, and every push overwrites name from here, so this is the only place two sittings can ever be told apart. `planPush` asks whenever a sitting EXISTS rather than when one has results — the tracker cannot know a conduct happened until the Evalbee sheet arrives days later, so results lag the conduct and keying on them leaves a silent-overwrite window. **`paperSittings.ts`** is the round-trip over `paper_pushes` (`listPaperSittings`/`recordPaperSitting`, idempotent by the DB's unique key so a double-click cannot mint two exams). Numbers are allocated server-side from freshly-read rows — the client sends an INTENT (`new`|`update`), never a number, so a stale tab cannot name a sitting already taken.

Pure validators (`isValidEmail`, `isValidPassword`, `isValidRole`, `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 8`) plus DB-side `listMembers / createMember / resetMemberPassword / removeMember / updateMemberRole`. All five DB helpers take the caller's `orgId` explicitly and use `createSupabaseAdminClient` (service-role) — `auth.admin.*` needs it; `org_members` reads gate on admin RLS anyway. `createMember` reconciles against existing auth users (email-taken-other-org / already-member / new-user paths) and stamps `user_metadata.name`. `removeMember` + `updateMemberRole` enforce last-admin protection AND self-protection (caller can't remove or demote themselves). All helpers return discriminated-union results consumed by `/api/admin/members`.

The RPC takes every list as a parameter (kinds, `hasMocks` exams, campaign, the chapter-test exam) so the SQL holds nothing that can drift; the ONE rule it duplicates is the onboarding arm split, and `tests/growth-snapshot.integration.test.ts` asserts it matches `onboardingArm()` on real ids. Specs `tests/growth-registry.test.ts` + `tests/growth-snapshot.test.ts`; `npm run growth:smoke` drives the loader live.
│   ├── pmf/acquisition.ts + migration 0125 `get_acquisition_snapshot`   **"Where students come from" on /dashboard/pmf (2026-09-28).** First-touch channel had been stored on `student_profiles.acq_*` since 09-17 (0106) with no page showing it. The RPC (SECURITY DEFINER, service_role only, 0103's posture) returns signups per (source, medium, campaign, before-tracking) and per first page, each with SIGNALLED (use, not reach: a mock started, notes progress, a bookmark, or a `user_activity` kind other than `surface_viewed`/`paywall_event`/`email_clicked`), MOCKED (submitted/expired) and PAID (Razorpay, not revoked); `p_exam` narrows to students who chose an exam. The pure core groups into display channels (ChatGPT · Google · Bing · Shared mock result · Other search · Social · Other websites · Tagged links · No referrer recorded · Before 17 Sep), keeps "no referrer" and "before tracking" apart and neither as "direct", withholds rates below `MIN_SEGMENT_N`, groups first pages by section + exam, and parses the page's `?acqDays=`/`?acqExam=` (filter links `prefetch={false}`). `describeSource` also gives /dashboard/students/[id] its "Came from" line. Specs `tests/pmf-acquisition.test.ts` + `tests/acquisition-snapshot.integration.test.ts` (counts, staff excluded, a page view is not a signal, the exam filter, anon/student refused); `npm run pmf:smoke` drives the loader on live data and checks landings add up to tracked signups.
│   ├── notes/{keepGoing,testBar}.ts + app/notes/_components/{NotesKeepGoing,NotesMockCard,NotesTestBar}.tsx   **the way on at the end of a notes page (2026-09-28).** Before it, a topic page ended with the practice wall, a /browse drill and (on 58% of topics) hand-written "Related notes" — no next topic, and the chapter menu sits behind a button at the TOP on a phone. `keepGoing.ts` (pure, registry-derived): `topicNav` (next/prev in `subtopicOrder`; a chapter's last topic → the subject's next chapter; the subject's last → `/notes/<subject>`), `chapterStart`, `mockCta(examName)` (only for a `hasMocks` exam; quotes NO free-mock number — a cached page cannot know the live limit), `extraRelated` (drops related links that repeat next/prev, anchors ignored — 279 of 602 did). `testBar.ts` holds the slide-up bar's rule (70% scrolled, once a day per device) APART from keepGoing because the bar is a client island and keepGoing imports the whole registry. The bar replaces a pop-up: a modal over a phone page is what Google demotes for search visitors. Clicks count as funnel events `notes_mock_card_click` · `notes_test_bar_shown`/`_click`. `tests/notes-keep-going.test.ts` also fails on any "Related notes" link to a missing notes page (10 CET links were 404ing).
│   ├── sites/imat/notes/                   **IMAT teaching notes for the future IMAT site (2026-10-09; NICHE_SITES_SPEC.md).** 46 chapters · 671 concepts across six subjects (biology · chemistry · physics · maths · logic · reading), one folder per chapter in the `/notes` data shapes so the shared notes components draw them. Registered ONLY in `registry.ts` (`IMAT_NOTES_CHAPTERS`, `IMAT_NOTES_SUBJECTS`), never in `NOTES_CHAPTERS`, so no PYQ Vault surface lists them; `tests/imat-notes.test.ts` fails if any file outside this tree and `/dashboard/imat-notes` imports them. `checks.ts` (`checkImatChapter`, pure) is the content contract: no `pyqExampleId` (every IMAT row is PRIVATE; 2011-2022 can never be published), a five-option self-check per concept (`AuthoredExample.options`, answer starting "(C)"), letters spread, ≥3 practice reps, ≥3 traps, KaTeX renders, tables only where BlockText draws them, true intro counts, no dashes. Every worked example and self-check is ORIGINAL; the past papers are evidence only. Read by `/dashboard/imat-notes` (superadmin preview, noindex). Authoring: `scripts/imat/notes/AUTHORING_BRIEF.md`.
│   ├── seo/{title,pageTitles,pageAudit}.ts   **page titles that fit a search result (2026-09-27).** `title.ts` — `fitTitle(lead, parts, {shortenLead?})`: ≤70 chars (Bing's "Title too long" line), keeps the lead + required parts, drops optional parts (last-listed first) and then the ` · PYQ Vault` brand, cuts the lead at its first ":"/"—" only when nothing else fits, and trims at a word as the last resort; returns an ABSOLUTE title (`title: { absolute }`, so the layout template adds no second brand). `pageTitles.ts` — `/questions` + `/board` chapter titles, using the registry `displayName` (and naming the board, which ended duplicate titles across CBSE/MH). `lib/notes/titles.ts` — every `/notes` title, checked across the whole registry for length AND uniqueness by `tests/notes-titles.test.ts`. `pageAudit.ts` — pure core of `npm run seo:titles` (`scripts/seo/titles.ts`): reads every sitemap page's head and reports long/duplicate titles, descriptions outside 25–160, noindex and non-200 URLs; `--site=http://localhost:3000` checks a local build before deploy. Triage, exits 0.
│   ├── dashboard/{stats,activity,countUp}.ts      getDashboardStats (totalQuestions/exams/chapters/daysSinceLastUpload/byExam) · getRecentUploads (cap 5; rows are <Link>'d to /uploads/[id]) · useCountUp hook (eased 0→target animation for stat numbers; respects prefers-reduced-motion). Pure interpolateCount + easeOutCubic exported for testability. (NB: this `dashboard/activity.ts` = admin stat cards; UNRELATED to `lib/activity/` the engagement spine below.)
│   ├── activity/{events,service,shape,adminStats}.ts   the engagement activity spine (migrations 0052/0053, 2026-07-12). `events.ts` = pure core (`ACTIVITY_KINDS` learning-anchored allowlist · `isActivityKind` · `sanitizeActivityEvent` bounded-payload validation · `buildActivityRow`, TDD). `service.ts` = `logActivity`/`logActivityBatch` — **best-effort**, swallows its own errors so a logging failure never blocks the primary action; emitted from `submitAttempt` (mock_submitted + per-miss answer_wrong, once-per-attempt behind the in_progress guard), the notes-progress route (chapter_mastered/note_checkpoint), and the bookmarks route (question_bookmarked); signed-in only. `shape.ts` = pure interpretation layer (`ActivityShape` type · `KIND_LABELS` · `classifyUsageShape` daily/burst/mixed/insufficient verdict, TDD). `adminStats.ts` = `getActivityShape` (service-role `get_activity_shape` RPC → typed shape). Backfill: `scripts/activity/backfill.ts` (idempotent via dedupe_key). **Reach instrumentation (2026-09-27, migration 0123):** `views.ts` (`SURFACES` · `viewDedupeKey` per user+surface+IST day · `surfaceViewedEvent`), `clientEvents.ts` (`parseClientEvent` — the closed client allow-list; `paywallEvent` builder with gate × step), `clientBeacon.ts` (`sendActivity`/`sendActivityOnce` → /api/activity/event, keepalive), `components/ViewBeacon.tsx` (a view from a CACHED page, e.g. /start); `service.ts` gains `logActivityOnce` (upsert on dedupe_key, ignoreDuplicates). Views are written server-side in /drill (+ `drill_started`), /me, /me/map, the result page, the mock start page (+ `paywall_event` shown when locked) and /pricing; the header's /api/me/pulse writes `surface='site'` once a day — the heartbeat. See [[project-engagement-engine]].
│   ├── students/{roster,rosterQuery,admin,derive,detail}.ts   the /dashboard/students engagement roster (migration 0098 `get_student_roster()`, 2026-09-15). `roster.ts` = pure core (TDD `tests/students-roster.test.ts`, 32 cases): `filterRoster` (exam ARRAY-overlap + an explicit `EXAM_UNSPECIFIED` bucket so the ~59 students who never named a target exam stay reachable · a `TimeWindow` DISCRIMINATED union all|days|never · a `DateField` toggle so the window can mean 'active in' or 'joined in') · `sortRoster` (**nulls LAST in both directions** — an ungraded student must not top a descending score sort; stable, non-mutating) · `summariseRoster` (fed the FILTERED rows so the stat cards follow the filters) · `toRosterCsv` + `formatMobile`. **The CSV writes a mobile as `91 9876543210` and the SPACE is load-bearing**: a bare digit string is read by Excel as a NUMBER and rounded to sci-notation — irreversibly and with collisions — which is exactly how the hand-made `generated-papers/nda-users.csv` destroyed every number; quoting does NOT save it. The leading `+` is dropped so the cell doesn't trip the formula-injection guard that protects the user-supplied `name`/`city` cells. `rosterQuery.ts` = `fetchStudentRoster(db)`, client-INJECTABLE (the itemStats precedent) so `npm run students:smoke` can drive it outside Next; it pages the RPC in 1000-row windows because a set-returning RPC is subject to the same PostgREST cap as a table select. `admin.ts` = the thin `server-only` service-role wrapper. `derive.ts` = `displayName`/`providerLabel`, shared with the mocks + feedback admin readouts. `detail.ts` = the per-student `[id]` page. Client `StudentRosterClient.tsx` does filter/sort/CSV in memory (the `LeadsBrowser` idiom), with `aria-sort` on each sortable header. Absence is null everywhere, never 0: `avgPct` null = no graded attempt, `lastActive` null = never acted (its own filter bucket, the re-engagement list). `last_active` is the last LEARNING action, deliberately NOT `auth.users.last_sign_in_at`.
│   ├── performance/{types,query,service,compute,conceptGraph,focusAreas,laneNav}.ts   the per-student performance diagnosis (migration 0099 `get_student_performance()`, 2026-09-15) behind `/dashboard/students/[id]/performance`. **The RPC RETURNS jsonb, not a table** — the heaviest student carries 4,500 facts and 2,091 distinct (attempt, subtopic) pairs, both past the PostgREST 1000-row cap that truncates silently; one jsonb row is immune (the `get_activity_shape` 0053 precedent). Measured on prod: avg 151 kB, p95 567 kB, max 1,086 kB, 235 ms, all shared-buffer hits. It **makes no judgement** — it joins and projects; right-vs-wrong is decided ONCE by `verdictFor` (`lib/mocks/answers.ts`), shared with `gradeMock` and the review page, because a second implementation in SQL is the two-renderer drift this repo has paid for twice (`scripts/itemstats/rollup-vault.ts` sets the read-rows-judge-in-TS precedent). It takes ONE REQUIRED parameter, since an optional `p_subject` would be the migration-0068 generic-plan anti-pattern. `facts` is built from the mock's question SNAPSHOT left-joined to `attempt_answers`, giving **FOUR response states, not three**: answered · seen-and-left-blank · **never reached** (no row at all) — only 240 of 606 graded attempts wrote a row for every question (avg 62.7% of the paper), so collapsing the last two reports "weak in Conics" for a student who ran out of time at Q80; the skip audit therefore counts seen-blank ONLY, and never-reached becomes the pacing readout instead. `compute.ts` = the pure core (TDD `tests/performance-compute.test.ts`, 36 cases): `selectAttempts` (in-progress excluded · FIRST sitting per paper, since 23% are retakes and the review screen shows the answers · `ENGAGEMENT_FLOOR` 0.2, matching rollup-vault — every exclusion is COUNTED, not just applied, because the page owes the reader "2 retakes excluded" rather than a total that silently disagrees with the roster) · `recencyWeight` (1 today, 0.5 at 30d, floored 0.2, **and clamped at 1** so clock skew can't weight one attempt above all others) · `consistency`/`stdDev` (**population sigma, matching nda-tracker** — the sample form moves real students across a label boundary, pinned by test) · `computeTrend` · chapter/subtopic roll-up · `buildProjection`. Lanes are keyed **(exam from the ATTEMPT, subject from the FACT)**, which is what splits an NDA GAT paper's lumped 100-question "General Knowledge" section into the 8 subjects it really holds — 103 students see one number today where the actionable fact is per-subject. Weightage is **derived live** from the bank's own PYQ counts and per-question marks come from the snapshot, NOT from nda-tracker's `NDA_FREQ_BY_SUBJECT`/hardcoded 0.33 penalty: those are hand-transcribed copies of this bank frozen at 2026-05-17, and MHT-CET has ZERO negative marking (five distinct schemes appear in ONE production payload). Every row carries `judged` + `thin` (`MIN_JUDGED_FOR_CLAIM` = 3) because the median attempt here answers 36% of its paper — the heaviest student's "33% History" rests on 6 answers of 173 — so a percentage never ships without its denominator; and a NEVER-ANSWERED chapter is sorted to the BOTTOM of the weakness list rather than floating to the top as "0%". `conceptGraph.ts` + `focusAreas.ts` = the one genuine import from nda-tracker (`CHAPTER_PREREQS`, faculty-curated and not derivable from any corpus) → root-cause + ready-to-learn, NDA-Maths-only, linking through our own `/go/learn` + `/go/practice` redirects; a chapter under `MIN_JUDGED_FOR_CLAIM` maps to null so it can neither be called weak nor named as anyone's root cause. `query.ts` is client-INJECTED (`npm run perf:smoke`), `service.ts` the thin `server-only` wrapper — since **migration 0110 it exposes THREE readers, and which one a route picks is the security decision**: `getStudentPerformance` (service-role, RLS bypassed, superadmin surfaces only), `getOwnPerformance` (the student's own JWT through the zero-argument `get_own_performance()` — no user id is passed and none CAN be, so there is no parameter to tamper with), and `getStaffStudentPerformance` (`get_student_performance_for_staff`, which runs the batch_enrollments→batches→branch_members join AS THE CALLER and raises 42501 when the answer is no; it returns null on that code rather than throwing, because a refusal that renders as a stack trace tells an unauthorized reader the student exists). **Authorization lives on the ROUTE, never in the core** — that is what makes the future student-facing version a new page reading the same helpers with the anon client. Node-vs-live-taxonomy is a PROD-CONTRACT suite (`tests/performance-concept-graph-taxonomy.test.ts`), since `npm test` targets the seeded test project whose Maths taxonomy is 3 chapters. **`laneNav.ts` (2026-09-15) owns which lane opens**, extracted from the route where it was an untested `lanes.find(...) ?? lanes[0]`: the default is now the exam of the most recent COUNTED attempt (`summary.latest.exam`), not the busiest lane — measured on prod, **3 of the 12 students who span more than one exam would land on the wrong exam** under the busiest rule. The exam ROW stays busiest-first and independent of selection, so it cannot reorder under the reader's cursor; the subject default stays busiest-first because one paper spans many subjects and recency cannot rank them; and a subject is matched WITHIN the selected exam so `?exam=NDA&subject=Physics` can never hand back a CDS lane under an NDA heading. **`buildProjection` runs at TWO GRAINS (2026-09-15, migration 0100).** `Projection.subtopicRows` is flat and CROSS-CHAPTER — "which subtopic anywhere is worth the most" is the question the card answers, and a per-chapter ranking cannot answer it; that ordering is the one idea taken wholesale from nda-tracker's ProjectedScoreCard. Both grains go through ONE `rowFor` closure, because two implementations of expected-marks would eventually disagree behind a single toggle, and chapter marks are the exact SUM of their subtopics' (asserted in the unit tests AND in `perf:smoke`, which throws on a mismatch against live data). Rows are driven by the BANK's subtopic list, not the student's, so a subtopic they have never been shown still appears — carrying its full marks as gap, the largest gap there is. Matching is on the (chapter, subtopic) PAIR: `subtopics_chapter_id_name_key` makes names unique only within a chapter, so nda-tracker's match-on-bare-name would collide here. Every projection row now carries `judged` + `thin` as well, because ranking is by MARKS and a row scored off one answer can still sort high. NDA Mathematics derives **111 subtopics** live — the same count as nda-tracker's hand-transcribed `NDA_SUBTOPIC_SHARES`, which is frozen at 2026-05-17 and covers that one subject. **`lane.time` (2026-09-15) is the time-analysis block.** It was built only after verifying the field is worth building on: across 300 submitted prod attempts `sum(time_spent_secs) / (submitted_at − started_at)` has **median 0.95 and never exceeds 1.01**, so per-question dwell PARTITIONS the sitting's clock rather than approximating it. Four second-buckets (correct/wrong/blank/unjudged) that sum EXACTLY to the total, classified identically to `addToTally` so the time card and the coverage card can never describe different papers; medians only, because dwell is wall-clock including idle and prod holds one question at **6,131 s**; and reached-but-zero-dwell rows (6.8% of answer rows) are EXCLUDED from every median and reported as `zeroDwell` — a row with no reading is a measurement gap, not a zero-second solve ([[default-becomes-assertion]]). `SubtopicRow.timedCount` is the denominator that gates the `slowest` list, since one 600-second question is an abandoned tab, not a slow chapter. **`links.ts` (2026-09-15) owns the links OUT of the page, and there are exactly TWO of them.** Link A — `browseExtrasHref`, `/browse?extras=<ids>` — opens the exact questions THIS student got wrong or skipped; Link B — `topicHref` — opens the topic in the bank. The audits and the chapter accordion take A, because a row only appears there when a wrong answer or a skip put it there. The PROJECTION takes B, because it is a marks-at-stake ranking derived from the bank's weightage and therefore includes topics the student has never seen: measured on prod, **2,484 of its 4,939 subtopic rows have `judged === 0`**, so an "open their mistakes" column would open nothing on half the card. `OPEN_LIMIT` is 50 because `extras` becomes a PostgREST `.in()` list that rides in the URL (a bare `Bad Request` at 833 ids elsewhere here), and `openLabel` DISCLOSES the cap — the first version read the full count while the href sliced 50, which never lied only because the measured worst case is 34 wrong / 33 skipped per subtopic. `topicHref` degrades one step at a time (subtopic → chapter → no link) rather than falling back to the unfiltered bank under a heading naming one topic; `(unclassified)` is minted by the RPC's `coalesce` and has no id by construction. **`taxonomyQuery.ts` resolves NAMES to ids** for the selected lane only — the RPC returns taxonomy as name arrays that the facts index into, which is what keeps a 1,270 kB payload from also carrying a uuid per dim entry — so this is a ~30-120 row read for one subject, independent of how much the student sat. Client-injected like `query.ts`; `taxonomy.ts` is the `server-only` wrapper and FAILS SOFT, because every number on the page comes from the RPC and a broken taxonomy read must cost the links and nothing else. Names being the join key is a real coupling — a chapter renamed since the attempt was sat silently loses its link — so `perf:smoke` REPORTS the resolution rate per lane rather than asserting it (a rename is a fact about the bank, not a bug here); it read **100% on every lane** at ship, NDA Maths included at 31/31 chapters and 111/111 subtopics. `SubtopicRow.seenBlankQuestionIds` mirrors `wrongQuestionIds` and carries the same exclusion the skip audit itself makes — seen-blank only, never never-reached — and `perf:smoke` throws if a row's id list and its count disagree, because the badge reads the count and the href reads the list.
│   ├── email/{recommend,templates,resend,service}.ts   outbound mock-recommendation email (migration 0059, 2026-07-15). **First email-dispatch capability in the project** — closes the ROADMAP "Custom SMTP (Resend) — now a real need" gap for this one campaign. `recommend.ts` = pure core (TDD `tests/email-recommend.test.ts`, 39 cases): `pickRecipients({students,mocks,attempts,priorSends,now})` → at most ONE email per student per run, two cohorts — **next_mock** (has attempted → newest unattempted mock in the same exam AND `paper_code`; scoping to the paper is what "the course they attempted" means, since every published mock is NDA so the exam filter alone is a no-op) and **first_mock** (never attempted → newest `FIRST_MOCK_PAPER` = maths, LWS's core subject). Skips: no email · **`isUndeliverable`** (RFC 2606/6761 reserved TLDs — `.invalid`/`.test`/`.example`/`.localhost` + `example.com|net|org`; leaked test fixtures live in auth.users and a hard bounce is what suspends a young sending domain) · opted out · account < `MIN_ACCOUNT_AGE_MS` (2d) · inside `COOLDOWN_MS` (7d — longer than the sibling app's 3d because a mock is a 150-min sitting) · mid-exam (a LIVE `in_progress`; an abandoned past-expiry one counts as attempted). Dedupe keys carry the anti-nag policy: `next_mock:{userId}:{mockId}` (never re-recommend the same mock → an ignored pick walks down the catalogue) · `first_mock:{userId}` (once, ever). `templates.ts` = pure (TDD, 22 cases): `buildEmail(recipient, token)` → `{subject,text,html,replyTo,headers}` — **always text AND html** (the sibling app's "biggest deliverability win"), `escapeHtml` on every interpolated value (`name` is OAuth-controlled), `List-Unsubscribe` + `List-Unsubscribe-Post` (RFC 8058 one-click), and **`REPLY_TO` → a monitored mailbox** (`EMAIL_FROM` is a send-only address with no inbox, so a student hitting Reply would otherwise bounce) **paired with a footer line that invites the reply** — a reply-to nobody is told about is dead weight; the sibling app invites replies because a student who can't sign in has no other channel. `resend.ts` = raw-fetch transport (no SDK — CLAUDE.md's dependency rule; `THROTTLE_MS`=600 for Resend's 2 req/sec free tier) + `emailEnv()` (`RESEND_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM`). `service.ts` = service-role reads, all **paged** (1000-row cap) + `ensureUnsubscribeTokens` (lazily mints a `student_profiles` row — 25 of 41 students have none — the token comes from the column DEFAULT) + `recordSend` (one immutable `email_sends` row per attempt, sent OR failed). `recommend`/`templates` are pure; `resend`/`service` are deliberately **NOT** `server-only` so the tsx script can import them (the `lib/quiz/assemble.ts` precedent — `server-only` is a Next build alias, unresolvable under tsx) and take the supabase client as a param. See [[project-mock-recommendation-email]]. **`mockReport.ts` + `mockReportService.ts` (migration 0110, 2026-09-18) are the per-ATTEMPT report**, built entirely from the 0099 payload so the email costs one round trip and shares `verdictOfFact` with the page rather than re-deriving correctness. The grain SPLITS by necessity: per-question sections (`easyWrong`, `easyLeft`) are scoped to THIS attempt because one question is a fact needing no sample size, while `subtopics` is POOLED from the projection — one paper gives a CHAPTER a median of 3 questions and 29% exactly one, at or under `MIN_JUDGED_FOR_CLAIM`. `easyLeft` counts REACHED blanks only (never-reached becomes `pacing`, floored at 5: only 19% of production's easy-skips were ever looked at, so the naive version shows questions the student never saw), ranks by DWELL rather than peer evidence (a 90s abandon is a decision, a 1s pass is not), and the subtopic picks are scoped to the SUBJECTS THIS PAPER HELD and floored at the paper's own modal per-question marks — live samples otherwise recommended Synonyms after a Mathematics paper and ranked a 0.8-mark subtopic on an exam that pays 2.5 a question. `readPeerAccuracy` pools `question_item_stats` at READ time (36% of mock questions have evidence; an absent question stays OUT of the map, never 0, so "no evidence" and "nobody got it right" remain distinguishable). Runner `scripts/email/send-mock-report.ts` — dry-run default, FORWARD-ONLY via a `SINCE` const, 3/student/day cap, `--sample-to` for a reviewer copy that writes no row. **`click.ts` (2026-09-27, migration 0122):** `newClickToken` (minted BEFORE the send, because the row is written after Resend accepts), `clickUrl`, `resolveClickTarget` (same-site only); `templates.ts`'s `ctaHref` routes every CTA through `/api/e/<token>` when the builder input carries `clickToken` (all four senders pass one — the three crons and `send-next-mock.ts` via `recordSend`'s fifth argument; samples do not). `dueNudgeService.countClickedSends` joins `email_clicked` rows to send ids in one pass (no `.in()` over ids), and both `--report` scripts print `clicked`. **`signedLink.ts` + `signedLinkService.ts` (2026-09-30):** the redirect SIGNS THE STUDENT IN on a link under `SIGNED_LINK_DAYS`=7 old — `decideSignIn` (pure, TDD `tests/email-signed-link.test.ts`: never over a live session, not even another account's; fail-safe on a bad stamp) → `signInFromSend`, an admin `generateLink({ type: "magiclink" })` verified in the SAME request by the SSR client, the user looked up by id FIRST because `generateLink` CREATES an unknown email (integration `tests/email-signed-link.integration.test.ts`). The outcome rides on the `email_clicked` row as `metadata.signIn` (done · failed · expired · session-present); the route's test replaces `next/headers` with an in-memory jar to prove the session cookie lands. `tests/email-links-tracked.test.ts` is the CONTRACT that every content link in all four templates goes through `ctaHref` (the unsubscribe pair exempt) — the welcome's three step buttons did not until 2026-09-30, so its "0 clicks" had measured only the grey footer line.
│   ├── push/{core,browser,client,service}.ts + api/push/subscribe + public/sw.js + public/manifest.webmanifest + public/icons/push-*.png + scripts/push/send-due-nudge.ts   **browser push for the due nudge (2026-10-01, migration 0128, PUSH_SPEC.md).** `core.ts` pure (TDD `tests/push-core.test.ts`): `parseSubscription` (https endpoint ≤2048, base64url keys, UA cut to 256) · `pushTitle` ("N mistakes are waiting in Fix", ≤34 chars — the email subject truncates on a collapsed lock screen) · `drillTimeLine` (min(total,5) minutes) · `buildDuePushPayload` (≤3 chapters then "+K more", url through `clickUrl`, BookOpen `PUSH_ICON`/`PUSH_BADGE`) · `serializePayload` (<3500 bytes) · `classifyDelivery` (2xx sent · 404/410 gone · else failed) · `shouldDropAfterFailure` (`PUSH_MAX_FAILS`=5). `browser.ts` holds `isIosNotStandalone` + `urlBase64ToUint8Array` APART from core because core reaches `node:crypto` via `lib/email/click.ts`. `client.ts` ("use client", every Notification/serviceWorker access guarded) — `pushAvailability` · `subscribeToPush` · `unsubscribeFromPush` · `currentSubscription` · `stampPushPrompted`. `service.ts` (paged service-role reads, client as a param so tsx imports it) — `readSubscriptions` · `readPriorPushSends` (the email's PriorSend shape: push sends use the email's own dedupe key, so both logs feed one `selectDueNudges`) · `recordDelivery` · `readPushConversion`. `selectDueNudges` gained `channel` + `pushUsers` (email skips `has-push`; push picks only subscribers and ignores email consent). `/api/push/subscribe` POST/DELETE: session check, then a SERVICE-ROLE upsert on `endpoint` so a shared browser changes hands (integration `tests/push-subscribe-route.integration.test.ts`); RLS `tests/push-rls.test.ts`. UI: `mock/attempt/[attemptId]/result/PushOptIn.tsx` (the once-only ask; renders its WhatsApp-card children when this browser cannot push — `lib/profile/push.ts` `resultPageAsk`, TDD `tests/push-ask.test.ts`) + `account/PushCard.tsx` (this browser's state); both take `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` as a prop and render nothing without it. `/api/e/[token]` falls back to `push_sends` and records `push_clicked`. Sender `npm run push:due-nudge` runs before the email in `due-nudge.yml`; `--self-test=<email>` writes no row.
│   ├── upload/{parser,validate,hash,taxonomy,commit,subjectAliases}.ts   upload pipeline (pure → DB). parser reads optional Excel columns Q (question_number), Course, Set; commit stamps upload_job_id + pyq_year/pyq_month/pyq_note + question_number + set_id (`<uploadJobId>:<setLabel>`, scoped per upload) onto every inserted row. Taxonomy auto-create runs in two parallel pre-resolve passes (unique chapter keys, then unique subtopic keys) before the row loop; resolveChapter/resolveSubtopic catch 23505 and re-find the winner's id so concurrent commits adopt rather than fail. `taxonomy.findSubject` prefetches all subjects for the examId into a normalized-name map and walks `subjectAliases.subjectMatchKeys` to resolve common variants (Maths ↔ Mathematics ↔ Math, Physics ↔ Phy, Chemistry ↔ Chem, Biology ↔ Bio) without forcing teachers to match the canonical spelling exactly.
│   ├── upload/{detectCourse,resolveExam,propagateSetContext}.ts   pure helpers run by /api/upload/preview before validateRow. detectCourse → none|uniform|mixed across rows' "Course" column. resolveExam combines the detection with the form's optional examId and the live `exams` list to either resolve a single examId or return a row-level error. propagateSetContext walks rows in order, fills blank Question Context on set siblings from the first non-empty row of the set, and reports row-level errors on drift (different non-empty contexts within a set) or empty (no row in the set has Question Context).
│   ├── upload/{uploadDetail,deleteUploadJob,setUploadPyqMetadata}.ts   /uploads/[id] read aggregate (per-question rows + pyq aggregation: value | null | "mixed") · whole-upload delete (cascades + storage cleanup) · partial PATCH of pyq fields (explicit null clears).
│   ├── sites/nicheExams.ts               exams that belong to a NICHE SITE, never to PYQ Vault (NICHE_SITES_SPEC.md; IMAT first). A NAMED list, not "not in EXAM_REGISTRY": /browse keeps an exam ingested before anyone registered it. `listExams` (the /browse exam dropdown, the one PYQ Vault surface listing every DB exam) drops them via `withoutNicheExams`. Becomes part of the site registry when P1 is built. Spec tests/niche-exams.test.ts. Also: `StoredOptionLabel` (A-E, migration 0140) in upload/validate.ts is what commitStaged stores; the Excel template's `OptionLabel` stays A-D.
│   ├── uploads/listUploads.ts             paginated upload-jobs query for /uploads index. Uses `count: "exact"` to return `{ items, total }` in one round-trip; recovers from PostgREST 416 (over-the-end range) via a HEAD-count fallback. DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE = 20.
│   ├── guide/{buildBrowseUrl,principleSlug,resolveTaxonomy,loadWorkedExamples}.ts   server-side helpers for the /guide tree (also reused by /notes). `buildBrowseUrl(partial)` wraps `buildSearchParams` for every "Drill the N questions →" CTA (accepts `extraIds` so principle drills + per-concept notes drills can union subtopic match + curated UUIDs; a `kind` field — pyq/practice/all, added 2026-06-17 for the `/go/practice` redirect; also accepts non-filter `from`/`fromLabel` appended after the filter query — powers the /browse `BackToNotes` pill). `principleSlug(name)` → URL-safe slug with Greek-letter transliteration (π → pi, ω → omega) for /principles/[slug]. `resolveTaxonomy(client, examName, subjectName)` returns `{ examId, subjectId, chapters: Map<name, { id, subtopics: Map<name, id> }> }` so editorial content can stay env-agnostic. `loadWorkedExamples(client, ids)` fetches a curated UUID list into WorkedExample rows (text/**context**/difficulty/solution/chapter/subtopic/options + a **`provenance`** string composed via `formatProvenance` from question_number/pyq_*/exam — both added 2026-05-29 so set-bound PYQs rendered inline keep their passage and every card shows a `[Q# · month · year]` citation); RLS-respecting and drops unresolved IDs silently.
│   ├── notes/{chapters,splitNoteIntoSlides,pickInterleavedCheckpoint,deriveSummary,conceptWeight,taxonomyCache,subtopicSlugRegistry,loadResolvedDrills,pyqDuplication}.ts   server-side helpers for the /notes tree (`pyqDuplication` = pure `numberMultiset` + `reusesPyqNumbers` behind notes-lint check #5; unit-tested):
│   │       · `chapters` — the `NOTES_CHAPTERS` registry (single source of truth for shipped chapters) + `getNotesChapterBySlug` / `getNotesChaptersForSubject`. Each `NotesChapterRegistration` carries examName, subjectName, subjectRoute, **subjectDisplay**, chapterSlug, chipLabel, chapter (ChapterNote), notes, slugs, plus the optional paywall fields **`tier?: "free"|"paid"` + `paidScope?` + `previewConceptCount?`** (2026-06-01; default free) — enough for the shared page renderers to build everything.│   │       · `pickInterleavedCheckpoint(drillsByConcept, conceptOrder, count=5)` — pure, deterministic round-robin pick across concepts for the per-subtopic mastery checkpoint (TDD in `tests/notes-pick-interleaved-checkpoint.test.ts`).
│   │       · `deriveSummary(note)` — pure; pulls every concept's formula + reference table + trap titles into `{formulas, traps, references}` for both `SubtopicSummary` and `ChapterRevisionSheet` (TDD in `tests/notes-derive-summary.test.ts`).
│   │       · `conceptWeight` — pure `buildConceptWeightTable(subtopics, chapterTotalPyqs)`: groups concepts by subtopic, sorts high-yield-first (zero-tag foundations sink), computes each concept's share of chapter PYQs. Powers the `ConceptWeightTable` chapter-overview triage table (TDD in `tests/notes-concept-weight.test.ts`).
│   │       · `taxonomyCache` — exposes `getNotesTaxonomy(client, examName, subjectName)`, a process-level memoised wrapper around `resolveTaxonomy` keyed by `(exam, subject)`. Survives between requests in the Node server; bypassed in SSG (each path renders once at build). Restart `npm run dev` after a taxonomy rename to bust.
│   │       · `subtopicSlugRegistry` — aggregates `{subtopicName → subtopicSlug + concept list}` across all chapter notes data, **auto-derived from `NOTES_CHAPTERS`** (today: Statistics + Vectors + Probability; adding a registry entry is enough). Exposes `getSubtopicNotesEntry(name)` for the edit page UI and `validateConceptTag(name, slug, conceptSlug)` for server-side tag validation in applyEdit.
│   │       · `loadResolvedDrills(client, subtopicSlug, concepts)` — Phase 2 reader: calls `loadConceptDrills`, filters each concept's `pyqExampleId` out of its drill list, sorts the remaining UUIDs ascending for stability, returns Map<conceptSlug, string[]> (omits concepts with zero remaining tagged questions).
│   ├── tags/conceptTags.ts                helper API for `question_concept_tags` (migration 0021). `getTagsForQuestion`, `setTagsForQuestion` (diffs added/removed/kept; idempotent on no-op), `getQuestionIdsForConcept`, `loadConceptDrills` (subtopic-scoped, conceptSlug → questionIds[]; the base query under `loadResolvedDrills`). All RLS-respecting.
│   ├── tags/principleTags.ts              helper API for `question_principle_tags` (migration 0023, parallel to conceptTags). `getTagsForQuestion`, `setTagsForQuestion`, `getQuestionIdsForPrinciple`, `loadPrincipleQuestionIds` (batch slug → questionIds map; powers TOP_11 live counts on /guide and the `?principle=<slug>` /browse filter). All RLS-respecting; same horizontal-vs-vertical wall as conceptTags (see Design axes).
│   ├── exam/                              Tier 1 IA helpers (2026-05-18). `examContext.ts` exports the `EXAM_REGISTRY` (slug + display name + per-exam route map) + pure resolvers (`resolveBankHref`, `resolveGuidesHref`, `resolveNotesHref`, `getActiveTab`, `isExamSlug`, `getExamBySlug`). **As of 2026-06-08 every exam's `notesPath` is its per-exam hub `/notes/<slug>`** (NDA → `/notes/nda`, MHT-CET → `/notes/mht-cet`, JEE → `/notes/jee-mains`) — so the Notes tab always lands in the right exam (JEE shows "coming soon" rather than NDA). `guidesPath` still `/guide/nda` for NDA, null for MHT-CET + JEE (→ `/guide`). The `/browse` exam filter is DB-driven so JEE appeared there automatically. `loadActiveExamContext.ts` is the server-only orchestrator: reads `qb_exam` cookie (RFC-safe underscore name) → validates against registry → fetches exam UUID via cookie-free anon client → returns `{slug, examId, bankHref, guidesHref, notesHref}`. `examHomeStats.ts` is the per-exam head-count helper for `/nda` (avoids the PostgREST 1000-row trap; returns `{examId, totalPublicQuestions}`).
│   ├── exam/examFeed.ts + exam/examLinks.ts + viewer/useExamFeed.ts   **exam tiers (2026-09-25, EXAM_TIER_SPEC.md).** Every `EXAM_REGISTRY` entry carries a REQUIRED `tier` (`school`·`senior`·`graduate`). `examFeed.ts` is the pure core: `resolveStudentTier` (stated stage wins, else majority of targets, tie → first), `resolveExamFeed` (targets first, then the tier; the rest in `other`; never `noPublicContent`), `ANON_FEED`, and `splitByFeed` (a family is primary when ANY member is). `examLinks.ts` = per-exam destinations that only go to a page with content (`mockCatalogueHref`, `notesHubHref`, `yourExamLinks`). `useExamFeed` returns `ANON_FEED` until `/api/me/header` answers, so first client render = cached server HTML. Tests: `exam-feed`, `exam-links`.
│   ├── exam/cutoffs/                      Published NDA & NA cut-off marks, both sittings 2016-2025 (20 sittings, no gaps), transcribed from the twenty UPSC cut-off PDFs (2026-09-14). `types.ts` is the shape + pure readers (`cutoffFor`, `writtenCutoffRange`); `nda.ts` is the data + `getSitting`/`latestSitting`. **A cut-off is a LIST of (audience, marks) pairs, not a number** — UPSC published ONE figure per stage for every sitting through 2025-I and then SEPARATE male and female figures for **NDA II 2025** (304.90 / 358.84 written, 666.01 / 737.40 final), which is also the first sitting with decimals; a `written: number` column cannot hold it, and flattening to the male figure discards a 54-mark gap. Nineteen sittings carry one `"all"` entry, the twentieth carries two, so the next split sitting needs no schema change. `cutoffFor` falls back to the `"all"` entry (a caller asking for the female cut-off of a 2021 sitting must get the published number) but THROWS when asked for a single `"all"` figure from a split sitting — there is no honest answer and inventing one is the flattening the shape exists to prevent. **`subjectMinimumPct` is per-sitting, NOT the constant 25% every source quotes**: four sittings published 20% (2016-II, 2022-II, 2023-II, 2024-I). **Eight sittings carry `recommended`/`vacancies`/`wings` = null, not 0** — UPSC adopted the "Recommendation Details" layout for the I-sitting in 2019 and the II-sitting in 2021, and a 0 would assert nobody was recommended. `airForceShape` flags `lumped` (2019-I, 2020-I, 2021-I print one Air Force line) vs `split` (Flying / GD-Tech / GD-Non-Tech from 2021-II), because the wing table is NOT comparable across that seam. Evidence lives in `scripts/nda-cutoffs/sources.json` (the extracted text of each PDF + its SHA-256); `tests/nda-cutoffs.test.ts` (34 tests) reconciles the corpus BOTH ways, pins the four 20% sittings and all four 2025-II figures by name, and asserts every claimed number appears in its OWN source text — fault-injected to prove it bites. No page or DB table: 20 rows changing twice a year.
│   ├── links/                             Backlink resolution shared between /browse and /guide. `notesIndex.ts` is the SOURCE-OF-TRUTH `getNotesChapterEntry` (derived from `NOTES_CHAPTERS` registry) used by both `getQuestionResources` and the CHAPTER_TABLE notes chip on `/guide/nda-maths`. `tagNames.ts` exposes pure `getPrincipleName(slug)` (from TOP_11) + `getConceptName(subtopicSlug, conceptSlug)` (from NOTES_CHAPTERS). `getResourceTagsForQuestions.ts` batches per-page principle + concept tag fetches (2 parallel SELECTs against the indexed tag tables, dedups input ids + output tags). `questionResources.ts` is the pure mapping `(examName, subjectName, chapterName, subtopicName, tags?) → {guide, notes}` consumed by QuestionList — chapter→playbook + subtopic→notes by default; tag-specific overrides ("Lever: <principle>" / "Concept: <concept>") when DB tags resolve. The 6 chapter-keyed subjects route via a `CHAPTER_KEYED_GUIDES` registry (2026-06-01) — adding one is a one-row change; Maths (principle override), English (subtopic-keyed) and the single-page landings stay special-cased. `tests/cross-link-integrity.test.ts` locks this routing + the reference-table `playbookSlug`→catalog links.
│   ├── notes/chapters.ts                  `NOTES_CHAPTERS` registry — single source of truth for the 20 shipped notes chapters (16 NDA Maths + 2 NDA Physics + 1 MHT-CET Maths + 1 NDA Biology; run `npm run stats` for the live list). 7 consumers derive from it: `subtopicSlugRegistry`, `links/notesIndex`, `links/tagNames`, `app/sitemap`, the per-subject `page.tsx` chapter cards, **`notesNav.ts` (per-exam hubs)**, `scripts/notes-lint`. Adding a new chapter = write `_data/` + clone the 2 route wrappers + append ONE entry (a new SUBJECT also needs its `<subjectRoute>/page.tsx`). A `tier:"paid"` chapter additionally needs its `[subtopicSlug]` wrapper to be `force-dynamic` (notes-lint guards this — see 2026-06-01 Decisions log). Refactored 2026-05-18; was previously a 5-file ritual.
│   ├── notes/notesNav.ts                  Pure cross-exam nav model (2026-06-08): `getNotesExamGroups()` (exams with ≥1 notes subject, registry order) + `getNotesExamGroup(slug)` (one exam, empty-subjects = coming-soon, null = unknown) + `notesExamSlugs()`. Groups `NOTES_CHAPTERS` by `examName` → subject (route/display/chapter+subtopic counts). Drives `/notes` index, `/notes/[examSlug]` hubs, and the sitemap. TDD `tests/notes-nav.test.ts`. **The `/notes/[examSlug]` hub lists every chapter (2026-10-07):** subject tabs, most past questions first with a Start-here pick (`_components/NotesHubChapters`), and a signed-in `ContinueReadingCard` reusing the Your-notes progress read; pure core `lib/notes/examHub.ts`. Notes trails now carry the exam crumb (`notesExamCrumbs`, `lib/notes/breadcrumbs.ts`).
│   ├── notes/goLinks.ts                   Pure resolver for the cross-app `/go/*` remediation redirects (2026-06-17): a slug-keyed + (chapterName,subtopicName)-keyed lookup over `NOTES_CHAPTERS` → `getSubtopicBySlug`/`getSubtopicByName`/`buildLearnPath` (slug-or-name → /notes path + concept anchor) + `canonicalSubjectName` (Maths→Mathematics alias) + `corpusForSubject` (Maths→practice / else→pyq). No DB; the `/go/practice` route layers a `resolveTaxonomy` call on top to turn names → /browse UUIDs. TDD `tests/go-links.test.ts`.
│   ├── relevance/{config,fit}.ts          **cross-exam syllabus-fit screen** (migration 0062, 2026-07-27) — "can a student taught the NDA ∪ CET syllabus solve this JEE Mains question?". `config.ts`: `AUDIENCE` ('nda-cet'), `JEE_MAINS_EXAM_ID`, `BLOCKING_TOOLS` (the out-of-syllabus technique catalogue: similarity/conjugation · 3×3 Cayley-Hamilton · eigenvalues · 3×3 nilpotent shift · the XᵀAX skew test) + **`REVIEWED_CHAPTERS`** — the chapters actually adjudicated, pinned **by UUID** (the /notes+/guide taxonomy joins go by NAME and rot on a rename; a UUID can't drift). The reviewed scope lives here and NOT in the DB precisely so *passed* and *never looked at* stay distinguishable — the exclusion table only knows about drops. `fit.ts` (pure, TDD `tests/relevance-fit.test.ts`, 14 cases): `parseFit` · `isFitExam` (the filter is meaningless off JEE) · `unreviewedChapterIds` · **`fitCoverage(fit, chapterIds)`** → `inactive | full | partial | unscoped`, which the UI renders as a caveat so an unscreened chapter can never read as vetted. See [[syllabus-fit-screen]].
│   ├── reports/                           Question-reports feature (migration 0024). `types.ts` exports the `ReportCategory` + `ReportStatus` enums, display labels, validators, and length caps. `createReport.ts` (client-bound, RLS-scoped) — validates input, resolves question.org_id (RLS-bound lookup → 404 if can't see), INSERTs with denormalized org_id, returns discriminated union (ok / duplicate_open_report on 23505 / question_not_found / invalid_category / invalid_details / error). `listReports.ts` — admin triage list with joined question metadata (PostgREST nested arrays flattened); RLS scopes via `(reporter sees own OR admin sees own-org)`. `updateReport.ts` — admin status transitions; auto-stamps `resolved_at` + `resolved_by` on terminal statuses, clears on revert.
│   ├── notes-reports/                     Concept-reports feature (migration 0028 / 2026-06-02), parallel to `reports/`. `types.ts` — `ConceptReportCategory` (incorrect-content / confusing-explanation / typo-or-formatting / broken-visualization / wrong-example / other) + labels + validator; **re-exports the `ReportStatus` bits from `reports/types`** (status lifecycle is shared). `conceptIdentity.ts` — flat registry built from `NOTES_CHAPTERS`, `getConceptIdentity(subtopicSlug, conceptSlug)` resolves the globally-unique slug pair → full identity (exam/subject/chapter/subtopic/concept names + route segments) or null. `createConceptReport.ts` — validates the slug pair (unknown → reject), resolves org_id via `resolveTaxonomy` → a PUBLIC question in the subtopic (taxonomy is org-less, so bank content is the org anchor), INSERTs denormalized breadcrumb + route segments. `listConceptReports.ts` — no joins (all denormalized). `updateConceptReport.ts` — mirrors `updateReport` against `concept_reports`.
│   ├── books/                                  the `/books` pipeline. `verify-curation.ts` exercises the curation SERVICE against live rows and **round-trips every operation** (fingerprint → apply → assert → invert → require the fingerprint back byte-identical), which is what makes it safe to run on production — and necessary, since the controls sit in a client component on an auth-gated `force-dynamic` page that `next build` never renders and nothing headless can click. `sync.ts` (`npm run books:sync`, **dry-run by default**) assembles a book's contents into `book_questions` from the derived order and keeps them in step with the bank; it is idempotent (a second run adds 0). `smoke.ts` (`npm run books:smoke`) drives the PAGE'S OWN loaders against the live bank — it exists because every /books route is auth-gated and `force-dynamic`, so `next build` NEVER executes one and a green gate proves only that they compile. It reconciles TOC-vs-chapter counts, section order, set contiguity, chronology, and that no set groups two different passages.
